====================================================================================================
1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY & RESEARCH HIGHLIGHTS
====================================================================================================
This document aggregates the complete findings of the 14-day empirical research pipeline
evaluating security vulnerabilities in AI-generated code. Using a novel Three-Pillar validation
methodology combining Multi-Tool Static Analysis, Formal Verification (CBMC/KLEE), and Dynamic
Fuzzing/Execution (AFL++, MSan, Atheris, Taint Tracking), we systematically isolate static noise
and compute authentic, empirical ground-truth vulnerability rates.

Key Findings:
  1. Out of 2,236 analyzed programs, only 7.02% (157 programs) are multi-pillar confirmed vulnerable.
  2. Static analysis tools exhibit an 83.86% novel false-positive rate (717 out of 855 static findings
     failed to be verified by formal models or dynamic execution).
  3. Dynamic execution revealed 273 dynamic-only vulnerabilities completely invisible to static signatures.
  4. LLM self-assessment exhibits an 87.72% overconfidence error rate, claiming vulnerable code is safe
     with ~88% stated confidence.
  5. Formal symbolic execution seeding (KLEE) accelerates fuzzing by 3.77x and yields +15.55% edge coverage.
